  with a nudge to check it against the bar or a garage door). Checked in
  the harness: a 20 × 40 ft driveway drawn into fake tiles measures
  800 SF. **Google is a look only** — its terms don't allow keeping or
  tracing its imagery, so it is proxied with no caching and never saved.
  Keys stay on the server (`/api/aerial/tile`, `/api/aerial/google`,
  `/api/jobs/[id]/aerial`, session client): `MAPBOX_TOKEN`, optional
  `ESRI_API_KEY` (without it the public World Imagery tiles), and
  `GOOGLE_MAPS_GEOCODE_KEY` with the Maps Static API on.
  **Esri's grey tiles (2026-10-03):** where Esri's public tiles stop
  (often zoom 19) it answers with a small "Map data not yet available"
  picture. `fetchTile()` asks with `blankTile=false` and treats a tile
  under 6 KB as that placeholder, then draws the tile's box with Esri's map
  export, which uses the sharpest photo it has there — enlarged, not a
  sharper photo. Not testable from the agent container (map hosts are
  refused); check it on the preview.
  **Suggestions while typing (2026-10-03):** the address box offers up to
  six matches a quarter second after the last key (`/api/aerial/suggest`:
  Mapbox autocomplete when `MAPBOX_TOKEN` is set, else Esri's public
  suggest, resolved by its key when picked; nearest the map first; nothing
  stored). The drawer's subtitle names the place the map is on. Not built: the
  photo's date (Esri's metadata service), and Dusty outlining the
  driveway himself — when he does, it is a dashed suggestion Coy accepts.
  **Budget** — moved to the job's **Summary** as **From the plans**
  (Coy, 2026-10-06: "this budget tab shouldn't be here since we already
  have a budget page"; the Plan Room is Files · Takeoff, an old
  `?step=budget` opens it as usual): each group and the line that counts it,
  priced by the engine's `budget()`, each shape's share as a child row, with
  Send to budget (`BudgetStep` / `SendSheet` in `plan-room/budget-step.tsx`),
  shown only when the job has takeoff groups. (Until 069,
  **Send to budget** picked a Price book card and added a `from_takeoff`
  line; that line rule still works and Dusty's plans use it.)
  **Sections follow the Plan Room (migration 069, applied 2026-10-03 on
  Coy's "Apply 069").** Coy: *"Pushing to budget should push the area
  measured under the group. And I should be able to select the individual
  measurements … Section should follow any edit in the plan room … if I
  delete the measurement, it should delete it in the budget without deleting
  the whole section because i wouldn't want to lose all the cost items … I
  should be able to pull measurement into the budget from the budget page
  also."* `takeoff_measurements.section_id` (on delete set null) puts a shape
  in a budget section; `job_sections.takeoff_sync` (default on) says the
  section's size follows its shapes; `job_files.takeoff_measurement_id` (on
  delete set null) files a photo on a shape. Triggers keep all three on one
  job. **The size is the sum of the section's shapes** in the first shape's
  unit — area → `sqft_override` (and the groups' depth when they agree),
  linear → `count_qty` LF, count → `count_qty` EA; a shape of another kind
  is kept but not added, and a shape on a sheet with no scale leaves the size
  blank with the reason (`sectionTakeoff()` / `sectionPatch()`,
  `src/lib/takeoff-sections.ts`, tested). It is **written on the server**
  after every takeoff change — a shape drawn, re-shaped or deleted, a group's
  depth, a sheet's scale (`syncTakeoffSections()`) — so the engine, the
  Dashboard, Dusty and the proposal read the section as before. A shape
  deleted takes its share out; a section left with none has its size
  cleared and **keeps its cost items**. A size typed on the section turns
  the following off (✎ "size typed here" · **↺ follow the takeoff**).
  **Send to budget** now opens the group's shapes with a tick each (those not
  in a section yet ticked) and makes **each its own section**, **all in one
  section**, or adds them **into a section** already there, with a kind of
  area guessed from the group's name (`/api/jobs/[id]/takeoff/send`); the
  group row says "N of M in the budget" and a shape in one shows **$✓**. On
  the job page each section has a **Takeoff** strip — its shapes and total,
  ƒ follows / ✎ typed, and **Pick takeoffs** (every shape on the job by
  group; ticking one in another section moves it here; one kind per section;
  `PUT …/sections/[sid]/takeoff`) — and sections sit under their takeoff
  group's name, as in the Plan Room. Cost items go on afterwards, as before.
  **Editing a shape:** with Select, a picked shape shows its corners — drag
  one to move it, the dot mid-edge adds one, double-click removes one; the
  section follows. **Shape photos** on the toolbar (with a shape picked) ticks
  the job's photos of that area or takes new ones; they show under the
  shape's section and on the proposal. Checked in the harness: Send made one
  section per shape, a dragged corner saved the moved point (800 → 1,148 SF),
  a ticked photo was filed on the shape. **Checked again (2026-10-03):** a
  section made by Send now fills from Coy's auto-loading assembly for that kind
  of area, like one added by hand (it didn't); and a group's depth only fills a
  blank thickness — one typed on the section stays (a re-measure used to write
  the group's over it). Rehearsed (rolled back) before
  applying: a shape or photo on another job's section refused, the following
  on by default, a stranger sees nothing.
  **A colour per takeoff (migration 070, applied 2026-10-03 on Coy's "Apply
  070").** Coy: *"the ability to select different colors for each
  measurement in the parameters panel … The colors should show different on
  the drawing as well. The colors don't need to move over to the budget, it's
  just for the plan room."* `takeoff_measurements.color` (nullable `#rrggbb`,
  checked; null = the group's colour, as before). The colour bar on each
  takeoff's row in Parameters opens its swatches (`SHAPE_COLORS`, "group"
  first) and the drawing — fill, outline, corners — uses it (`shapeColor()`,
  `shared.ts`); a colour alone doesn't re-sync sections. The budget, the
  proposal's area maps and the sheet viewer keep the group's colour.
  Rehearsed (rolled back): a hex kept, anything else refused. Checked in the
  harness: the Apron recoloured blue on its row and on the sheet.
  **Tick to send (Coy, 2026-10-03: "a check box next to the measurement in
  the plan room parameters so that I can select the measurement and push to
  budget").** Each takeoff's row has a tick box; ticking any (one group at a
  time — a section takes one kind) turns the panel's button into **Send N to
  budget**, which opens the Send sheet with just those ticked. Checked in the
  harness: ticking Apron sent only Apron. **On the job page** the section's
  row is **Measurements** with a real button — **+ Pull measurements from the
  Plan Room** (Change measurements once it has some; Measure in the Plan Room
  › when nothing is measured yet) — Coy couldn't find the old "Pick takeoffs"
  link.
  **Perimeter of an area (Coy, 2026-10-04: "calculate the linear footage
  around the perimeter of that area").** Every area shape also gives its
  edge in LF — its outline closed, plus each cutout's outline (a hole is
  edge to form too) × the sheet's scale, never stored, none while the scale
  isn't set (`measurementPerimeter()`, `src/lib/takeoff.ts`, tested). Shown
  as "N LF edge" under the group's SF / CY and each takeoff's SF, and on the
  shape's floating label ("971 SF · 180 LF perimeter"). A budget line counted
  from an area group whose card is in LF takes the group's perimeter
  (`ruleQty()`, `perim_lf` on the takeoff total) — form & finish by the foot
  from the same outline. Dusty's job context carries it per group.
  Checked in the harness on a generated sheet: a 30×40 ft rectangle at
  1/8" = 1'-0" is exactly 1,200 SF (14.8 CY at 4"), $2,467 on a $1.43 card at
  15% O.H. and 20% margin; a 10×20 apron moved the line to 1,400 SF / $2,878;
  taps ~16 ms. Dusty's job context includes each sheet's scale and each
  group's total. APIs: `/api/jobs/[id]/plans` (GET), `…/plans/sheets`
  (POST, the browser's reading), `/api/plan-sheets/[id]` (PATCH scale /
  number / title), `/api/jobs/[id]/takeoff/parameters[/pid]`,
  `…/takeoff/measurements[/mid]` — session client, org from the session,
  every id checked against the job; triggers keep a shape's group and sheet
  on one job, and a sheet's file on its job.
- **Job › Proposal (migration 060, 2026-10-01; Claude Design,
  `design/proposal`).** Coy: *"Go ahead with the proposals."*
  `/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/proposal`, from the job header's **Proposal**
  button (`src/components/v34/proposal/`). Left, the job panel (a sheet on a
  phone): project type (switching loads that type's defaults, asking first),
  the option, cost → price by cost type with the **margin** (slider and box —
  it writes the job's `estimates.margin_pct`, so budget and proposal never
  disagree), "Calculated $X → rounded to $Y", the sub split (20/80, 30/70 —
  never on the document), and the warranty and contract pickers. Middle, the
  document — always white paper, whatever the theme: Cover (a photo from the
  job's files), Header, Scope (intro + each area's size and spec), Pricing,
  Payments, Warranty, Terms, Sign — each with an on/off pill; quick toggles
  Item prices · Qty · unit · Tax line; groups and lines shown/hidden, prices
  hidden, lines made optional. **The cover's dark band (Coy, 2026-10-04):**
  the logo on the left, the kind and title on the right, nothing else — the
  "Prepared for …" line was repeated below. **Since 2026-10-05 the band is
  its own strip** between the Cover and the Header (Coy: "The cover should
  not include this dark header. I don't want to lose this when I turn the
  header off"): the Cover switch hides only the photo, the Header switch
  only the quote number and Prepared by / for, and the band always carries
  the logo. In print the cover photo is an `<img>` (3.4 in tall), the band
  keeps its colour (`print-color-adjust: exact`) and nothing forces a page
  break after the cover, so photo, band and header open page 1 together
  with a photo or without one. **Who it goes to, and Dusty beside each part (Coy, 2026-10-10, three desktop notes).** The Preview's
  **Send to** lists the job's person, the customer's other people (`GET /api/jobs/[id]/proposal/recipients`,
  session client) and, under **Your team**, the org's members (`sc_org_people`; a member who is also a
  contact, an onsite estimator, carries that contact's phone), each ticked or not, the job's person
  ticked to start. **Send text** sends one text each through Quo (`sendProposal()` per person, the
  greeting turned into each one's first name with `greetingSlot()`); a member with no contact has no
  phone to text. **Email it** opens one email to every ticked address, its greeting with no name when
  there are several. **Edit with Dusty is the emblem** (`DustyMark`, `document.tsx`) beside the scope
  introduction, beside each area's name (its scope of work, saved to the section's `prep_notes` as the
  budget's emblem does), and on the warranty and terms. Every such question asks Dusty to put the words
  for the page in a ```` ```proposal ```` block (`USE_ASK`, `src/lib/dusty/use-block.ts`, tested); the
  chat draws it as a highlighted box with **Use this …** inside it, and only the box is copied
  (`splitUse()`); an answer with no box is used whole, as before.
  **Prepared by** is the member preparing it — their
  name and phone from Settings → Account → You (`PATCH /api/me {phone}`, kept on their
  own account), then the org; a final freezes them as
  `snapshot.prepared_by`, and the customer's copy carries name, phone and
  email only. **Prepared for** shows the phone as (225) 555-0100 and the
  email (the contact's, else their first listed one) when there is one.
  **Preliminary (Coy, 2026-10-07: "I need to make sure it's clear that this
  is a preliminary estimate").** A **Preliminary** chip beside Item prices
  (`display.preliminary`, no migration; a new version keeps it): the band
  and the quote's name read "Preliminary estimate", and a line above the
  price says the final price is confirmed after a site visit and exact
  measurements. It reaches the customer's link (`publicSnapshot()`), and
  Dusty's proposal context says whether it is on.
  **Maps & photos per area (2026-10-03, Coy:
  "the customer can easily understand what this section is covering. They
  will have the map view + photos for that map area"):** under the Scope's
  list, each area with takeoff shapes shows the part of their sheet — the
  aerial or a plan page — with the shapes drawn on it (drawn in the browser
  from the plan with pdf.js, `area-visuals.tsx`), beside up to four of its
  photos (filed on the area or on one of its shapes); an eye hides one area's,
  the **Maps & photos** quick toggle hides all (`display.picsOff`,
  `hide_area_pics`). Read live from the Plan Room, like the cover photo — a
  final shows today's shapes for its areas. **Since 2026-10-05 the photos are
  thumbnails** (76px square on a phone, 92 on a desktop), each opening full
  size, and a section's **Photos** row on the job page has a **Proposal**
  switch that is that area's eye (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id]/proposal {pics:
  {section_id, on}}`, read with `GET …/proposal?display=1`, which makes no
  draft; with none, the switch opens one). A link made without the browser
  (sent from Threads or Dusty) now takes photos filed on an area's takeoff
  shapes too, as the Preview does. Warranty and terms are edited in place;
  **Save as template** writes them back (same name updates it, a new name
  makes one). **Edit with Dusty** opens him with a starter question, and
  **Use this** under his answer (`setDustyUse()` in `dusty-live.ts`) puts it
  in the section — Coy's tap; Dusty writes nothing. **Print / PDF** is the
  browser's print (`@media print` shows the pages only); a PDF filed on the
  job needs a server renderer — later. **Preview** (it was Mark final —
  below) freezes the document when it is sent, in `job_proposals.snapshot` (the priced document and the warranty and
  terms as worded), refused while draws don't add up; the database refuses
  any change to a final (`sc_job_proposal_guard`); **New version** starts
  the next draft. One draft per job.
  **The proposal's number (Coy, 2026-10-10: "SE-26-0001-A (company-year-proposal
  number-project letter)").** SE is the org's initials from `orgs.name` (the first
  letter of each word, letters only, LLC / Inc and the like left out, four at most;
  none → P); 26 the year, Central time, the job's FIRST proposal was made; 0001 the
  job's place among the org's jobs whose first proposal was made that year, in the
  order those were made (same instant: by job id); A the version — v1 A … v26 Z,
  v27 AA (`src/lib/proposal-number.ts`, tested in `tests/proposal-number.test.mjs`;
  `proposalNumbers()` in `proposal-number-server.ts` reads `orgs.name` and every
  `job_proposals` row's job and `created_at` under the session). **No column, no
  migration:** proposals are never deleted (a job is archived, its proposals stay),
  so a job's place never moves. Renaming the org changes the initials on what is
  read, not on what was sent. It replaces `<job_number or P>-<version>` ("P-3")
  everywhere a number is shown: the proposal's own row and version picker, the
  document of a draft, Preview and its link list, Files' Proposals, the Summary's
  proposal and Won cards, Threads' proposal picker and "/" links, Dusty's proposal
  and Proposals to send lines, and the activity line's "Proposal opened / signed:
  SE-26-0004-C · …". **A final freezes its number in `snapshot.number`** (as
  before): the customer's link, its tab title and the document of a final show the
  number it was sent under, so the finals frozen as "P-1" … "P-3" before this keep
  them for the customer; inside the app they read the new number with "sent as
  P-3" beside it (Dusty: "sent to the customer as P-3"), and Files finds either.
  Measured 2026-10-10: Wilson Driveway is SE-26-0004 (its v4 draft SE-26-0004-D),
  Sacco Driveway Repair SE-26-0010-A. `doc_templates` were seeded from the
  design's text (from Coy's won quotes) as **starter** text — "{org}" is the
  org's name, the license number a blank — marked "check it with your
  attorney" until Coy saves his own. Dusty's job context has the proposal
  (total, rounding, what's hidden, draws) and never makes a draft. APIs:
  `/api/jobs/[id]/proposal` (GET, PATCH), `…/final`, `…/new-version`,
  `/api/project-types[/id]`, `/api/doc-templates[/id]` — session client, org
  from the session, every id checked. Rehearsed: one draft per job; a final
  with no snapshot, or an edit to a final, refused; a stranger sees 0 rows
  and can't write; anon refused.
- **Preview and send — the proposal by link (migration 075, Coy
  2026-10-03: "Mark final should be changed to: 'Preview' … the preview of
  the proposal that the customer would see when I send it … send the proposal
  via text and email … track every time the customer views the proposal …
  hosted on the cloud").** The proposal toolbar's **Preview** (Mark final is
  gone, and so is the "Fix the budget first" button — Coy: "doesn't even
  work"; why it can't be sent is said in words on the Preview) opens the
  document exactly as the customer gets it — read-only, hidden sections,
  groups and lines gone, no empty cover, no draw checks — laid out for the room it has
  (the phone layout under 600px, the desktop one above; no Phone / Desktop
  switch since 2026-10-11, Coy: "one proposal that will adapt"), with **Text it · Email it · Copy link** (`preview.tsx`).
  Sending a draft freezes it as a final version first (the same server check
  as before); then its **link** is made: a `documents` row (the old empty
  placeholder for sending quotes) with `proposal_id`, a 32-character random
  `token`, `shown` — the customer's copy, built by `publicSnapshot()`
  (`src/lib/proposal-links.ts`, tested) WITHOUT cost, O.H., profit, margin,
  hidden lines or groups (not even their names), hidden prices (zeroed) or
  the areas' notes — and `assets`, the storage paths of the logo, cover and
  each area's photos and map (the map drawn with its shapes into a JPEG by
  the browser, `mapPicture()`, and uploaded to
  `job-files/{org}/proposal/{doc}/`). Text and Email open **Coy's own**
  Messages or mail app with the words and the link (`sms:` / `mailto:`) —
  ScopeCrete sends nothing (§10); `sent_via` records which. A new version's
  link retires the old ones (the customer sees "replaced — ask for the
  latest"); **Turn this link off** revokes one. **Resending (Coy,
  2026-10-11: "I don't see a way to resend proposals. Also I'm not seeing
  the photos added to the section on the proposal links").** Once sent, the
  Preview's buttons read **Resend text** / **Email it again**, the toolbar's
  **Preview & resend**, and the job's Summary has **Resend** under the
  proposal (`?send=1` opens the Preview). A resend asks the server for the
  live link again, which re-reads each area's photos (filed on the section
  or its takeoff shapes, `areaPhotoPaths()`) into its `assets`, adds a map
  for an area that had none, and keeps the words and prices frozen; a signed
  link (097) is never touched — photos added after signing go on a New
  version. The server's photo read was also two `.or()` filters on one query;
  it is one now, with the area check in code. **An older final too (Coy, 2026-10-11: "I should still be able to copy a link and resend a proposal that was frozen").** An earlier final picked in the Proposal tab's version list keeps Preview & send (Resend) and Copy link, opening the same Preview for that version: the link route takes `{version}` and sends that final's own link (signed or not); one whose link was replaced is made the customer's link again, saying the newer one will show "replaced"; one older than a version the customer signed is refused, with **Open … to copy or resend it** taking Coy to the signed one. New version stays on the newest only. The customer opens
  **`/p/<token>`** (Vercel, public, no sign-in, `noindex`): `/api/p/[token]`
  calls `sc_shared_proposal()` — SECURITY DEFINER, the ONE thing anon may
  execute here — which returns `shown` for a live link only and logs a
  `viewed` event (a hashed IP, the browser) with `view_count`,
  `first_viewed_at` / `last_viewed_at`, unless the reader is in the org (Coy
  checking his own link isn't a view). Pictures are signed by the same
  request's client; storage policy `shared_proposal_assets` lets anon read
  an object only while a live link lists its path (`sc_shared_asset()`). **Sent from Threads (Coy, 2026-10-11: "Sending the proposal should open up the threads for that customer. I can email or text it from there").** The Preview's Send to picks one person (the job's person to start; the customer's other people, Coy's team); its button is **Text it in Threads** / **Email it in Threads** (with Copy link). It makes the link as before (a draft frozen first), fills the words, and opens the person's thread with them in the text box (the composer's own per-device draft, `sc:draft:<contact>`), or a new email to them (`/dashboard/messages/email/new?contact=&to=&subject=&body=`); Coy presses Send there — through Quo, or the two-tap email. The link is marked sent by the server when a text (`/api/texts`) or email (`/api/emails/send`) carrying a `/p/<token>` goes out (`markLinksSent()`, `linkTokensIn()`, tested; a signed link left alone), and the words are kept for learning when the thread opens (`openInThreads()`, `send-proposal.ts`). The Preview no longer texts directly or opens mailto; Dusty's send_proposal step and the "/" link are unchanged.
  Anon has no grant on `documents` or `document_events`; members read and
  write them under `get_user_org_ids()`; a link must point at a FINAL
  proposal on its own job (`documents_guard`). **Tracking:** the toolbar's
  line ("v1 sent · opened 3 times · last 2h ago"), the Preview's list of
  every opening with the device, and the activity line ("Proposal opened:
  Wilson Driveway Replacement"). APIs: `/api/jobs/[id]/proposal/link` (GET
  the job's links with their openings; POST the link for the newest final),
  `/api/proposal-links/[id]` (PATCH `{sent}` / `{revoke}`) — session client,
  org from the session, every file id checked against the job; `/api/p/[token]`
  — no service role. Rehearsed (rolled back): a member makes a link; one on a
  draft refused; anon reads the frozen copy and the view is logged once; a
  member's own opening not logged; a bad token null; a listed path readable
  by anon, an unlisted one not; anon can't read either table; a stranger sees
  no links and can't make one; a revoked link is gone and its pictures with
  it. **Applied 2026-10-04 on Coy's "apply 075"** (with anon's leftover
  grants on `contact_phones` revoked; checked after: the columns, the guard
  trigger, the storage policy, anon may execute only the two functions and
  holds no grant on `documents`, `document_events` or `contact_phones`, a bad
  token returns nothing). A preview deployment sits behind Vercel's own login, so a link made
  anywhere but localhost points at `https://scopecrete.ai` (`publicOrigin()`,
  2026-10-07: links made on the feedback preview were *.vercel.app, and Dusty
  hit Vercel's login trying one). Dusty is told never to open a link, and a job
  named in his question carries its budget (none, no items, or items and
  price — `jobValues()`), not "open the job for its budget". The customer
  approves and signs on the page since migration 097 (§6 Won); not built: picking an option there,
  and a PDF filed on the job.
- **The job's Bid strip (2026-10-01, §2i)** — full width like the section
  cards (2026-10-02, Coy: "needs to be the same width as everything else");
  folded by default since
  2026-10-02 (Coy: "doesn't take up a lot of space on my mobile phone") to
  one row, "Bid · due Tue, Oct 13 …", ▶ opens it, remembered per device. On a job a forwarded bid made
  or fed: GC, estimator, bids due (hours left, red under 48), pre-bid, the
  Box folder and what was copied and read, **Scope from the bid** (each
  item with its sheet, spec and line) and **Undo bid**. The Dashboard has a
  **Bids due** tile (open bid invites by due date).
- **Job header (2026-10-02, Coy's notes on the Burgess job):** ‹ Jobs and
  the job name on one row (bold, smaller), the four chips small on one row
  under it, the customer line and description (left out when the Bid strip
  below shows the bid's scope summary — Coy: "redundant"), then the job's
  switch above the money strip. **Delete is at the bottom of the job page**
  (Coy, 2026-10-04: on a phone it pushed Proposal off the screen), and the
  header's sheets button reads **Sheets (n)** on one line. **Since
  2026-10-05 all three views share one top row** (`JobHeaderRow`,
  `job-tabs.tsx`; Coy: "I like the layout of the budget page the best with
  the job name on the top left and the 'plan room, budget, proposal'
  buttons on the top right … Any buttons currently in the top right need to
  be moved down below"): ‹ Jobs, the job's name (and the ⓘ, or the
  proposal's number and draft / final, with its price line under it) on
  the left, the switch alone on the right. Each view's own buttons sit
  below (the Budget's Sheets (n) button is gone since 2026-10-06 — Coy: "accessible from within the plan room"; a section's own sheet links stay, and on a phone the job's name is 14px (`.sc-job-name`) and ‹ Jobs just ‹, so a long name fits the width); the Plan Room's
  reading chips on the row under the name;
  the Proposal's version, Item prices · Qty · unit · Tax · Maps & photos,
  Print / PDF and Preview on the settings row beside Project type,
  Warranty and Contract (on a phone, the buttons on their own row and the
  settings folded).
- **Summary, a fourth view (Coy, 2026-10-05: "a summary page to the left
  of the plan room … including all the chips" and "all of the lines and
  words directly below the job name … we can add to the summary page").**
  Summary · Plan room · Budget · Proposal, at `/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/summary`
  (`job-summary.tsx`): the four chips, then one fact a row — the customer, the
  address, "Created Oct 2, 2026", the number of sections (Coy, 2026-10-09) — the
  description (Why this job is gone — Coy, 2026-10-09: "redundant … the information exists right above it"; `GET /api/jobs/[id]` still returns `why`), the Bid strip, the price
  at a glance from the engine's `budget()` over `/api/jobs/[id]/budget`
  (total, cost, O.H., profit, margin, tax, job minimum, or what blocks it),
  the proposal's link (version, sent, opened —
  `/api/jobs/[id]/proposal/link`), and Delete job at the bottom (on the Budget's bottom too since 2026-10-09 — Coy couldn't find it after a New scope) (a deleted
  job opened there shows Restore — `GET /api/jobs/[id]` now returns
  `archived_at`). No new API. Dusty is HOT on the job there. An onsite
  estimator gets no Summary tab, and `redirectFor()` sends /summary to the
  job page. Under them, **From the plans** (since 2026-10-06, the Plan
  Room's old Budget step). The Budget's chip row, customer and description lines, ⓘ and
  Delete moved to Summary (the Bid strip stays on both); the Proposal's
  number, draft / final and price left the name's row for one line above
  its settings. **A job is renamed from inside it (Coy, 2026-10-10: "don't see a way to change the job name
  from inside the job"):** ✎ beside the name on Summary, Takeoffs and Proposal opens it as a box —
  Enter or leaving it saves (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id] {title}`, `JobHeaderRow`); the Budget's name was
  already typed in place. Not for an onsite estimator. **The Budget has no Bid box (Coy,
  2026-10-10: "it should be in the summary page")** — the Bid strip is on the Summary alone. **A
  section's row (same day):** its name is 13px, the size of the bid's scope line, not the display
  face; Dictate is the mic icon alone; the kind-of-area picker is gone from it (the kind stays on
  the section for assemblies, set when it is made); the fold button sits at the row's right end. **Later that day (Coy):** Measured by is the full width, each way its own colour (Length × width blue,
  Area only green, Linear ft orange, Count violet; `MODES`), with no "Measured by" over it; the
  Measurements words are gone — **Measure in Takeoffs ›** (or Pull / Change measurements) sits on the
  row under it, and a line of the shapes counted shows only when there are some
  (`SectionTakeoffStrip`). The section's **Budget** row (with **+ Add item** on it since 2026-10-10, Coy; it reads only "Budget · N items" since that evening — the cost, price and per-SF left it, Coy: "The other information in between can be removed"; each cost item under a group is set in with a "-" so it reads as part of that group) is bold 13px on a tint, set in with a rule at
  its left so it reads as part of the section, its fold ▸ at the right, + Add items from the Price
  book inside it; its groups and items are 11–13px, the prices no bigger. **On a phone the job page's
  own header slides away with the dashboard's** while the dark numbers strip stays at the top: the
  sticky wrapper (`.sc-job-sticky`) sticks the header's measured height above the screen under
  `html[data-head-hide]`.
  **A job opens on its Summary (Coy, 2026-10-09: "when I open a
  job, let it default to the summary page")** — from the Jobs board and table,
  customer and contact pages, the Map, Files, search, Dusty's job picker, the
  activity line and the Dashboard; a job just made (+ New scope, call notes)
  still opens on its Budget, and an onsite estimator is sent to the Budget.
  **On a phone** (Coy: "the job name in the center of the page … plan room,
  budget, and proposal button … center of the page below the job name") the
  name is centred on its own line (‹ Jobs at its left edge; the Budget's
  editable name centred too) and the switch centred under it; the desktop
  keeps name left, switch right (`JobHeaderRow`).
- **The job page gets the phone's whole screen (Coy, 2026-10-05: "when I
  scroll down let the main dashboard header disappear under the job page
  header").** On a phone, scrolling down the job page slides the dashboard
  header away (`useHideHeadOnScroll()`, `html[data-head-hide]` sets
  `--sc-head` to 0) and the job's own header and money strip stay pinned at
  the top; scrolling up brings it back. Desktop unchanged.
  **Every page since 2026-10-06** (Coy: "this page and any other page that I
  have opened … pushes up and covers over the main header bar"): the hook runs
  in the dashboard layout, reset on each page change, and a list page's own
  title row stays at the top as the list scrolls under it (`.sc-page-head` —
  Customers, Vendors, Contacts; the Jobs board's header; Threads' list header
  was already sticky). Pages that don't scroll the window (an open thread,
  Dusty) are unchanged. **A page with little to scroll keeps its header**
  (under 240px of room — a customer's Files tab jumped back and forth, Coy),
  and a scroll within 350 ms of the header moving is the page settling, not
  Coy (`use-hide-head.ts`). **Every page since 2026-10-10 (Coy: "consistent on every single page"):**
  a page that scrolls inside its own box (the contact card, `.sc-pinned`) slides the header away too —
  the hook hears scrolls in capture on the document, any scroller most of the screen tall, never one
  in a drawer, dialog or Dusty — and a record page no longer keeps it; its own sticky header rides
  up to the top. *Superseded:* **A record page keeps both (Coy, 2026-10-09:
  "the headers here in the customer page should be fixed and not slide
  up")**: the customer, contact and vendor page's header is sticky under the
  dashboard header (`data-head-pin` on `RecordHeader`), and the dashboard
  header doesn't slide away while one is on the page.
- **One ☰, one search (Coy, 2026-10-06: "use the same left slide panel
  hamburger menu button for anywhere there are selections that need to slide
  in … consistent on the left … in line with the page header" and "the same
  search icon and search bubble consistently across every page").**
  `MenuButton`, `SearchToggle` and `SearchBubble` in `primitives.tsx`, the
  look Threads' list had: the ☰ (40px, left of the title, on a phone) opens
  Threads' buckets, Files' folders and the Jobs board's stages; the
  magnifier (26px, in the title row, before + New) opens a full-width pill
  under it, × clears, Esc closes — Threads, Customers, Vendors, Contacts and
  Files. The Price book's Cards keep their search open (the budget's picker
  needs it), drawn as the same pill. Page titles on Threads and Files take
  `.sc-page-title` like the other lists.
- **The Map page (Coy, 2026-10-08, Claude Design 11: "add the map page to
  the main menu on desktop; toolbox on mobile … push the snipits from the map
  into the takeoffs tool … That can replace the current satellite page in the
  plan room").** `/dashboard/map` (`src/components/v34/map/`), after Jobs on
  the rail; in the Toolbox on a phone (`cleanMenus()` now puts a new page after
  its default neighbour, not at the end). **No map library**: a slippy map of
  our own (`map-view.tsx`) drawing tiles from `/api/aerial/tile`, which now
  takes `p=streets` (Mapbox Streets, `fetchStreetTile()`) and any zoom from 2
  — the token stays on the server; drag, wheel and pinch zoom about the
  pointer, double-click in. Web Mercator, scale and clip maths in
  `src/lib/map.ts` (tested, `tests/map.test.mjs`).
  **Pins** (`GET /api/map/pins`, session client, org from the session): every
  job not archived at its own address, else its customer's, coloured by layer
  — Active jobs (won, scheduled, in progress) · New leads · Estimating /
  Follow up · Proposals sent (a sent, live link; "Opened N times") · Bid
  invites (due, red under 48 h) · Complete and Lost (off by default) — and
  vendors at their contact's address as squares (`layerForJob()`). Layer
  switches (behind ☰ on a phone — no Layers button; Map · Sat sits in the
  header beside Clip, Coy 2026-10-09) with counts, job type, city and created filters (kept on the
  device, `sc.map.filters`), the legend, clusters below zoom 11, Map ·
  Satellite. A tap opens a card: Open job, Directions (Google Maps), Call,
  Send to Takeoffs; a vendor's: Vendor page, Call, Directions, Email. The
  search box finds addresses (`/api/aerial/suggest`) and the pins' own names.
  Customers have no pins of their own — they show through their jobs; the
  header's count says how many named customers have no address ("· 23
  unmapped"), with no banner or button about it (Coy, 2026-10-08: "You can
  remove this note"). **Points** are looked up with
  `findAddress()` (the satellite's geocoder), 30 a load, and kept on the row
  by **migration 090** (applied 2026-10-08 on Coy's "apply 090"; checked after: the four columns on both tables, both range checks, RLS unchanged) (`jobs` / `contacts` `lat`, `lng`, `geocoded_at`,
  `geocode_query` — the address looked up; a changed address is looked up
  again; a miss is kept so it isn't asked again). Until 090 is applied
  addresses are looked up on each visit, remembered by the server process, and
  the page says so.
  **Clip for takeoff** (the header's button; the phone's floating one; Send to
  Takeoffs on a card): satellite, a frame (up to 960 × 680 on a desktop, the
  width less 16px a side on a phone — Coy, 2026-10-08: "a little bigger") (dimmed outside, thirds, the scale
  "1 in ≈ 41 ft · zoom 20", a 50 ft bar, "imagery date not known"), **Use this
  frame** — which saves the clip there and then (its centre, zoom and size;
  the map is held still after it, so the job list can't change what was
  framed — Coy, 2026-10-08: on a phone the frame shrank for the list and cut
  off the driveway) — then **Which job?** (on a phone the whole screen under
  the clip bar, the saved frame as a thumbnail with its size in feet and
  ‹ Change the frame) — nearest first, search, or **+ New job here**
  (the street from Mapbox reverse geocoding, `GET /api/aerial/suggest?at=`,
  through `POST /api/jobs`). **Put it in Takeoffs** posts `/api/jobs/[id]/aerial`
  as the old tab did, now with the frame's size (`w`, `h` — 1,150 to 2,560
  pixels at the whole zoom saved, `clipPlan()` / `validClipSize()`, so a sheet
  is always a drawing) — the same one-page PDF with the map's scale for Coy's
  tap. A job's second aerial is "AERIAL 2", and so on (`isAerialSheet()`). Then
  "Aerial added to X · Measure it ›" opens Takeoff on that sheet, and the
  framed box stays on the map. Zoomed out past 16 the frame asks to zoom in.
  **On a phone since 2026-10-10 (Coy):** the floating Clip for takeoff button
  is gone (the header's Clip does it); the legend is an icon at the bottom left,
  closed until tapped; "17 pinned · 25 customers with no address" sits at the
  top of the Layers panel, not in the subheader; the Layers drawer opens over
  the search and every button on the map (the map's box is its own stacking
  layer). A search used to leave the whole map, its pins and the footer shifted
  sideways when the keyboard opened: the page, the map's box and its wrappers
  are `overflow: clip` (hidden boxes can still be scrolled), the window is put
  back to no sideways scroll whenever it moves, and the search box's text is
  16px.
  `?job=<id>` (Takeoffs → Files' **Pull satellite images**, under the drop box; Coy, 2026-10-10) opens on
  satellite with the job's address (its own, else its customer's — `GET /api/jobs/[id]/aerial`) typed in the
  search and found, pinned at zoom 19; Clip for takeoff then has that job picked. No address, or none found,
  says so under the search.
  `?addr=<address>` opens on that address, pinned (the contact card's Map button, 2026-10-10).
  **Not built:** vendors' delivery circles (their area is a line of text), a
  frame resized by its corner, customers' own pins, live directions or
  drive times.
  **The office and its radius (migration 098, applied 2026-10-10 on
  Coy's "apply 098"; checked after: RLS on, 2 policies, anon no grant,
  authenticated select/insert/update/delete, 0 rows; Coy: "set my office location in the settings of my organization …
  When I open the map, it should be centered on the office with a radius of
  … 80 miles").** `org_office` (one row per org: the address as the map
  spelled it, its point, `radius_mi` 0–1,000 or blank; members read, only
  owners write — `get_user_owner_org_ids()`; anon nothing). Settings →
  Organization → **Office and radius**: the street box finishing the address (Mapbox),
  Radius in miles, Save / Remove (`/api/org/office`, a typed address placed
  by Mapbox). The Map opens fitting the ring (else the office alone at zoom
  11, else the pins as before): a dark office marker, a dashed ring at the
  radius with "80 mi from the office" on its north edge, and a ⌂ button
  beside the map's controls that goes back to it (`ringPoints()` /
  `ringPx()` / `cleanRadius()` in `src/lib/map.ts`, tested). Nothing is
  filtered by the radius. Without 098 Settings would say the office
  waits for it and the Map would open on its pins.
- **The Scope Builder (Coy, 2026-10-09: "Inside a job, I've got the Scope
  Builder tool … it flows from files to budgets to takeoffs. To proposal …
  a dedicated page for the scope builder tool … separate from the jobs page").**
  `/dashboard/scope-builder` (`scope-builder.tsx`, `/api/scope-builder`,
  `src/lib/scope-builder.ts`, tested), on the rail before Jobs and in the
  Toolbox. Every scope not archived (Lost left out) with its flow — **Files ›
  Takeoffs › Budget › Proposal › Sent**, done steps ticked and the next one
  marked, each a link to that view — one line on where it stands, and its
  price from `jobValues()`. **Residential · Commercial & government** split
  the markets (job type public or commercial is commercial); a residential
  job measured in the field by section counts as measured without Takeoffs
  or files. Step chips filter by what each needs next; + New scope opens the
  Jobs board's New job form. Counts only, nothing written. A first cut for
  Claude Design to work up; the Jobs board stays the sales stages. Not for
  an onsite estimator.
**Coy's evening notes (2026-10-10).** The subheader's line is the org's name in Manrope 300 (the wordmark's "crete" grey), a small green dot, then the page; the ⓘ description is no longer drawn. The activity line drops its six dots, shows only background work (reads, files, bids — not Dusty's chat), in Manrope 500 at 12px in a softer green (#4DB88C), lower beside the ".ai"; a tap opens **Logs → Dusty** (`?tab=dusty`, `DustyLog`): the reader's warning, his thoughts (tap one to talk about it) and the last week's background work — the drop-down is gone. **Dusty left the dashboard header:** his emblem is a button on each page's subheader (`PageHeader dusty`, `DustyPageButton`, event `sc:dusty-toggle`), beside the job's tabs and on Threads' list header; his history rail opens to the left of his column on a desktop. His greeting's quick selects now cover a thread (New text, New email…), Threads, Scope Builder, Jobs, the Map, Customers, Vendors (Vendor, Receipt, Quote, Bill — each opening its form; `ReceiptImport kind`), the Price book (group, card, assembly) and the Roadmap. **A call is a bubble** on its side like a text (theirs left, ours right), with no Inbound/Outbound word; a call Quo marks `no-answer` (`call.completed` in the raw log, read by `/api/threads/[id]`) reads **Missed call** in red. The customer page's ⋯ has no Call notes; its facts have **Location** — Change / + Add with the Mapbox street finisher (`PATCH /api/customers/[id]`). A job's Summary sets its address the same way (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id]`). The bid request composer drops down under the header (`Drawer drop`, `.sc-drop-panel`) with a search over the jobs. The job's name is 16px with a rule after ‹ Jobs on a desktop. Every "colour" the app shows reads "color".
**A customer's or vendor's page header (Coy, 2026-10-11).** One line like the main pages' subheader: "‹ Customers • Arts Apartments • Overview" (a vendor's "‹ Vendors • … • Prices"), a green dot between each, the tab's word without its count (on a phone a tap on it opens the tabs, as ☰ does). The back link is always the list — never back to the thread — and there is no role tag after the name. A vendor's ☰ has no Call notes, and their Overview shows the address as a link over its satellite picture, like the customer's Location. The Price book picked from a budget has no ⓘ how-to, and its + Group / + Card sit on a row under the title. A vendor's Files tab on a desktop is the desktop Files view (its box had shrunk to its content in a flex column). The job Summary's **From the plans** reads a takeoff group whose shapes are in budget sections (069) as "in <section>", with those sections' items, their price and its price per unit — not "no budget line · no rate", which is left for a group in no section and counted by no line.
**Coy's phone notes (2026-10-11).** **Settings → Cost book** (`/dashboard/settings/cost-book`, `cost-book-settings.tsx`, a section in `settings-sections.ts`, not for an onsite estimator): **Import** — a spreadsheet (CSV, Excel, UFGS .sec) or a receipt, invoice or quote, the same `PriceImport` / `ReceiptImport` the Price book's Import ▾ opened — then the pricing defaults and project types that were the Price book's **Defaults** tab (Coy: "Move the import button to the settings page. Make a new page in settings for the cost book" and "Move the defaults tab to the settings"). The Price book is Cards · Assemblies; `?tab=defaults` goes to Settings → Cost book, and every "Price book → Defaults" the app said (the engine's blocks, tax, the budget, the proposal) reads "Settings → Cost book". The Price book's header is the search magnifier (its bubble under the header, the find row's box gone) and the emblem; **+ Group and + Card left the header** and are Dusty's quick selects **New group · New card**, opening the forms (`create`, `?new=group|card` / `sc:create`); a group's own + Card stays, and picking for a budget keeps both on its title row. **By group · By vendor sit on the Cards · Assemblies row** at its right on a phone too, the card count only on a desktop. **Customers' + New and Vendors' + Vendor and + Receipt or quote are gone** — the emblem's quick selects (New customer; Vendor · Receipt · Quote · Bill) open the same forms. Imports' Undo stays on Logs → Price book.
**Coy's desktop notes (2026-10-11, evening).** **Settings → Templates** (`/dashboard/settings/templates`, `templates-settings.tsx`, a section in `settings-sections.ts`, not for an onsite estimator): every contract and warranty (`doc_templates`, migration 060, `/api/doc-templates`) in a list; a pick opens it to rename, tick the project types it is offered for, change its title and its words (a warranty) or clauses (a contract, add and remove), Save, Discard and Delete (proposals already made keep their copy); + New per kind; **Ask Dusty** with the emblem asks him to rewrite what is in the editor, and Use this puts his boxed words in, unsaved. The proposal's settings row has **Edit templates ›**. The proposal's settings row has no **Project type** picker (the job's type is on its Summary); when the draft's type differs from the job's, a link offers to load the job's type defaults. Its two explanation lines are gone. **Start over** on the proposal's toolbar (`POST …/proposal/new-version {fresh: true}`, no migration — a final can't change and proposal numbers count every version, so nothing is deleted): every customer link on the job not signed is turned off (it reads replaced), and the open draft is reset to the job type's defaults, or a blank new version is made. The Scope Builder's market and step filters sit in a drawer behind ☰ left of the org's name, the ones in use as chips with ×; its + New scope is gone (Dusty's quick select). The job Summary's From the plans header reads "Takeoff parameters → group".
**Coy's desktop notes on the proposal and the job's tabs (2026-10-12).** The proposal's dark band sets the job's title at 20px (17 on a phone; it was 26 / 20). Above Scope of work a **Job information** line reads "Job site: <the job's address>" when the job has one (`vm.job.address`, so the customer's link and a final show it too). On a desktop the job's switch (Summary · Files · Takeoffs · Budget · Proposal) has arrows pointing right for dividers, each the previous tab's tip in its colour, so the row reads as the flow (`.sc-job-tab + .sc-job-tab::before`, `v34-shell.css`); the phone's tabs are unchanged.
**Deleting a proposal version (Coy, 2026-10-12: "How do I delete proposals?"; migration 104, written, NOT applied).** **Delete** on the proposal's toolbar acts on the version on screen (`DELETE /api/jobs/[id]/proposal/version?version=N`). A proposal's number counts every `job_proposals` row and a job's place comes from its first one, so nothing that would renumber another job is removed: **a draft over an earlier version** is deleted outright (the page goes back to the last final); **a job's only draft** is not (the button isn't drawn; Start over gives a blank one); **a final** is marked `deleted_at` / `deleted_by` (104), left out of the version list and every reader, and its customer link turned off (it reads replaced); **a version the customer signed stays** (no button, the route and the guard refuse). 104 also has `sc_job_proposal_guard()` let a final change only in `deleted_at` / `deleted_by`, refuse marking a signed one, and refuse any DELETE of a final. Until 104 is applied a final's Delete says it waits for it; drafts delete now.
- **One subheader on every main-menu page (Coy, 2026-10-06: "notice that
  the logs page has a defined sub header. Each of the different pages on the
  main menu need a consistent subheader under the main header").**
  `PageHeader` (`primitives.tsx`, `.sc-subhead` in `v34-shell.css`), from
  Logs: a white band the page's full width, kept at the top while the page
  scrolls (under `--sc-head`; its height is `--sc-subhead`, which Files'
  folder column sticks below); ☰ (phone) · the title (26px, 20 on a phone)
  and its count · the page's buttons on the right; one line on what the page
  is for; then the page's search bubble or filter rows. Dashboard, Jobs,
  Cash, Contacts, Customers, Price book, Vendors, Files, Logs and Roadmap use
  it (Files' description left its ⓘ for that line; Roadmap's paragraph is now
  the line). Threads keeps its own list header — its bucket name in colour
  and no description line (Coy, earlier). The Price book picked from a
  budget, and Files on a customer's tab, keep their own rows.
  **Since 2026-10-07 (Coy's notes on master):** the org's name (`orgs.name`,
  through `OrgNameContext` from the dashboard layout) is the band's big line,
  the page's title small and uppercase after it with its count, and the line
  on what the page is for is behind an **ⓘ** beside the title (`InfoTip`) —
  "remove this line" on Logs, Jobs and the Price book. Customers, Vendors and
  Contacts fill the width (no 1156px cap). The Price book's find, By group /
  By vendor, Show retired, Columns and its count sit on the same row as its
  Cards · Assemblies tabs — By group / By vendor and Columns at the row's right on a desktop
  (Coy, 2026-10-08).
  **On a phone (Coy, 2026-10-07: "there isn't enough room for Sacco Enterprises jobs … Sacco
  Enterprises | jobs, but the font will be much, much smaller just on mobile")** the org's name and
  the page's read on one line, "Sacco Enterprises | JOBS", at ~11px; the ☰ is 28px, the height of
  the bar's other buttons. A sheet's title over 18 characters is set at 19px on a phone so it fits.
- **The desktop rail folds to its icons (Coy, 2026-10-10: "a button … to reduce the menu panel to just the icons without the word descriptions. And if i hover over the icons it will show the word").** A button at the rail's foot folds it to icons and back (Collapse; 64px since the sidebar below, 56px before); hovering (or tabbing to) an icon shows its name in a tip beside it. Saved on the account (`user_preferences.prefs.rail_icons`, no migration) and set before first paint by `app/layout.tsx` as `html[data-rail="icons"]`; everything measured from the rail reads `--sc-rail-w`. The phone footer is unchanged.
- **The desktop menu is a sidebar, its icons in colour (Claude Design "Rail Icon Colors", Coy 2026-10-10: "redesign the menu on desktop and create an icon color picker panel").** One 40px row a page — the icon and its name (Barlow Condensed 600, 13px, uppercase) — 232px wide; the open page on the rail's active tint with the accent edge drawn as an inset shadow; **Collapse** at the foot folds it to 64px of icons (the fold above). Above it, **Menu settings** (sliders) slides a panel out from behind the rail (Coy, later that day: "a menu settings panel that slides out from the main menu") — the same editor as Settings → Menus (`/dashboard/settings/menus`), to reorder the menu and colour its icons, the rail changing as they are picked; × , Esc or a click on the page closes it (`MenuPanel`, `settings/menus-settings.tsx`; not for an onsite estimator). Each icon may take a colour from the book — Ink (none), Accent, Chalk blue, Green, Teal, Violet, Red, Amber, Unsorted orange — saved as a role, not a hex (`user_preferences.prefs.menu_colors`, `src/lib/v34/menu-colors.ts`, tested; no migration), so it follows every theme; the open page's icon is always ink, the name always the rail's ink. Never saved means the **Suggested** preset; **Mono** and **By kind** are the others. Picked in Settings → Menus: the presets, then nine swatches under each page. On the dark rails (Kraft Yard, Steel, Signal, Kraft (clean)) a colour is mixed 45% toward the rail's light ink so it doesn't sink into the rail (`--sc-icon-lift`; Design didn't draw these). **Admin** (Design's "1b Pulse" icon) sits between Logs and Roadmap for a platform admin only. **The phone keeps the icons' colours too (Coy, 2026-10-10)**: the footer's and the Toolbox's icons take the same book colour, the open page marked by its tinted pill; a page with none stays ink. Their labels are centred in their columns, a two-word one ("Scope Builder") set tighter so it fits on one line, wrapping centred on the narrowest phones (`tabLabel()`, `toolbox.tsx`; Coy, 2026-10-10).
- **Rail icons (Claude Design, 2026-10-10):** Dashboard is four tiles in a masonry grid and Scope Builder three steps joined into a flow, Jobs a clipboard with a tick and Roadmap a signpost with two boards (`NavIcon`, `primitives.tsx`; 1.5 stroke in `currentColor`, so every theme, the rail, the phone footer and the Toolbox follow). The landing page's phone demo footer uses the same Dashboard icon.
- **Initials squares in colour (Coy, 2026-10-06: "a variety of colors and
  we can go from there").** `initialsHue()` (`src/lib/v34/threads.ts`,
  tested) hashes a name into ten colours (`INITIALS_HUES`), so a person keeps
  theirs everywhere `InitialsBox` draws them (Threads, the record pages);
  mixed with the theme's card and ink so dark themes read. No name → the grey
  dashed #; the open one stays hi-viz.
- **Questions pinned on a sheet (Coy, 2026-10-10: "If I want to ask Jeremy a question about these
  sections I measured … where would he go to see my question … tag him and the communications would be
  pulled into the existing thread for that contact … he could open and see the full drawing and zoom
  into the areas of question"; no migration).** Takeoffs' toolbar has **Ask**: a click on the sheet (or
  **Ask about this** on a picked shape) drops a pin and opens a box — pick who (the job's person, their
  customer's people, the job's vendors, the org's members; an onsite estimator is one person with their
  contact), write the question, **Send**. Each person gets it as an **internal note on their own thread**
  (`contact_notes` with the job), "📍 AERIAL 2 · Curbs 2: @Jeremy … Is this 6" or 8"?" and a last line
  `Open on the sheet: /dashboard/scope/<job>/plans?step=takeoff&sheet=<id>&pin=x,y&z=3&m=<shape>`
  (`src/lib/plan-pins.ts`, tested; `GET/POST /api/jobs/[id]/takeoff/comments`, session client, every id
  checked). A teammate is tagged in it, so 081's trigger writes their notification and the activity line's
  "Tagged you on …" opens the link — an onsite estimator, who can't read notes, sees their own pins from
  those notifications. **Also text them the link** sends it through Quo on the same tap (§6 Texts send
  through Quo), a full link to the org's address. The link opens Takeoffs on that sheet, zoomed in, the
  shape picked, scrolled to a ringed pin with its notes. Pins on a sheet are numbered; a tap opens the
  spot's notes, **Open their thread ›**, and a reply goes to the same people. Thread notes show "Open on
  the sheet ›". An answer comes back as their text or call on the thread — not on the pin. Not built: an
  estimator replying on the pin in the app (needs a `plan_comments` table with their access, a
  migration), resolving a question, email, phone push, and pins on the job's sheet viewer;
  Claude Design brief at /mnt/project-files/takeoff-comments/claude-design-brief.md.
- **Takeoffs, and back to the customer (Coy, 2026-10-07: "let's rename this tab page instead of
  plan room. Let's call it takeoffs. I'm going to create a dedicated plan room page for general
  contractors to upload plans and see quotes").** Every label that said Plan room / Plan Room says
  **Takeoffs** (the switch, links, the estimator's page name, Dusty's prompts); the address stays
  `/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/plans` and the code keeps its names. On a phone its **Files · Takeoff**
  hang under the Takeoffs button (`subnav` on `JobHeaderRow` / `JobTabs`; since 2026-10-09 the step reads
  **Measurements**, and its line drops from the button's centre and splits to each tab — Coy), not on a row of their
  own. A job's **‹** goes to its customer's page on the **Jobs** tab
  (`/dashboard/customers/[id]?tab=jobs`; "‹ Customer" on a desktop) — the Jobs board when it has
  no customer, or for an onsite estimator.
  **The job switcher (Coy, 2026-10-08: "Jobtread has a job switcher panel that slides in on the
  right side … accessed by clicking on the job at the top. The job name needs to look
  clickable").** The job's name at the top of every job view is a button with a ▾ (underlined on
  hover); on the Budget, where the name is typed in place, the ▾ beside it. It opens a panel on the
  right: every job not archived, newest first, with its customer, city and stage, a search, the
  open job marked; a pick opens the same view on that job (`job-switcher.tsx`, `GET /api/jobs` —
  RLS decides, so an estimator sees theirs).
- **One job, three views (Coy, 2026-10-02: "no way … to get back from the
  proposal page" and "one job budget page, and I can toggle between that
  budget and the proposal").** **Plan room · Budget · Proposal** — in the
  order of the work (Coy, 2026-10-03: "Plan Room, Budget, Proposal. That is
  the work flow"); the same
  segmented switch (`job-tabs.tsx`) on the job page, the Plan Room header
  and the proposal toolbar, so each is one tap from the others. The
  proposal's slide-over **Job panel is gone**: the money moved to the
  Budget (margin and O.H. already there; **cost by type** as one bar and the
  **profit split with a sub** (since 2026-10-10 a **Commission cost** — 0 ·
  20 · 30 · 40 · 50%, 0 by default, the partner's share of the profit: "partner
  $X · you keep $Y"; Coy: the 20 / 80 split gave the sub 80%. Kept as
  `display.commission_pct` on the draft — `split`'s check allows only the old
  three, so no migration; an old 20_80 / 30_70 reads as 20% / 30%,
  `src/lib/commission.ts`, tested; `PATCH …/proposal {commission}`; the
  customer's copy never carries it) — saved on the proposal draft, never on the
  document, never changing the price — in the budget footer,
  `PriceSummary`), and what shapes the document stays on the proposal as a
  **Proposal settings** strip (project type, option, warranty, contract;
  folded on a phone, open on a desktop). The proposal's sub-line says the
  total and "rounded up to …", or why there is no price.
  **On a phone the four views are plain tabs across the full width** (Coy,
  2026-10-09: "not buttons but just tabs") — the open one in ink with an
  accent underline (`.sc-job-tabs` in `v34-shell.css`); a desktop keeps the
  segmented switch.
- **Each section's plan sheets (Coy, 2026-10-02: "I need to verify the
  scopes on the sheets the scopes were taken from … expand and view each
  sheet … on desktop, a panel to the left or right of the budget").** Under
  a section's bid line, **Check it on A2.1 · A2.2** — the sheets its line
  names, linked only when a sheet by that exact number was read off the
  plans (`sheetsFor()`, `src/lib/section-sheets.ts`, tested: A2.1 is never
  A2.10). A tap opens the **sheet viewer** (`sheet-viewer.tsx`): on a desktop
  a panel over the right ~58% of the page — the page itself narrows to the
  room left of it (`html[data-sheets]`), so the budget stays in view, and
  with Dusty docked it sits between the page and him — with the sheets down
  its side: this section's, the other sections', then the rest of the set
  — each sheet once, in the first group that names it (several sections
  share a sheet; the first build listed them over and over);
  on a phone the whole screen with the sheets as chips. Fit · − · + (ctrl +
  wheel), drag to pan, ‹ › and the arrow keys. **Measure in the Plan Room ›**
  opens Takeoff on that sheet (`?step=takeoff&sheet=`) — measuring stays
  there, with its scale rule. The job header's **Plan sheets (n)** opens the
  same panel on every section's sheets. Attaching a sheet a bid line didn't
  name needs a place to keep it (a column on `job_sections`) — not built.
  **The takeoffs show on the sheet (Coy, 2026-10-10: "why doesn't it show me the drawings I made
  on the plans?").** Every shape drawn on the open sheet in Takeoffs is drawn over it, each in its
  own colour (`shapeColor()`), in an SVG laid on the page in page points; **✓ Takeoffs** in the
  viewer's bar hides them. **And onto a section as a picture (same day: "pull that photo into the
  section with the lines I've drawn").** A section's Photos → **Copy** opens with **Takeoffs · the
  sheet with your markups**: each sheet or aerial with shapes on it (this area's first, aerials
  before plan pages; a preview of the part that holds them, on a phone only for aerials — a scanned
  page is decoded whole to draw any of it) and **+ Add**, which draws that part of the sheet with
  every shape in its colour into a JPEG (`sheetMarkup()` / `mapPicture()`, `area-visuals.tsx`) and
  saves it as a photo on the section (`files`, category photo, "Takeoffs on AERIAL · Oct 10.jpg") —
  under the area here and on the proposal like any photo. It is a picture of that day's lines;
  re-measuring doesn't redraw it. The job page's **+ Add a section** is the width of its words on a
  desktop.
  **Where on the sheet (Coy, 2026-10-09: "verify … where the sections are on the plan set sheets
  that the job scope was pulled from").** A section's **From the plans** box sits at the top of its
  card — its sheet buttons and the bid's line — and **See where on the sheet ›** opens the viewer
  with that line boxed wherever it is written on the sheet (`findOnSheet()` /
  `quotedLine()`, `src/lib/find-on-sheet.ts`, tested: the sheet's own text runs from pdf.js,
  `pageText()`; runs within a few lines of each other are one label; a place needs half the
  line's words; a drawing that labels it several times gets a box at each). **Zoom to it** /
  **Next** steps through them; a line not in the sheet's text says so (drawn, scanned or on another
  sheet). Scanned sheets' OCR words aren't searched yet. The old "Check it on" row under the
  photos is gone (Browse the set stays when no sheet matched).
- **Dusty reads the plans' words (2026-10-02).** Asked to check each scope on
  its sheet, he answered that the plan files were "not read" — he had only
  their names. His job context now carries **What the plans and specs say**:
  the bid reader's text of every page that mentions concrete, labelled
  `[file · p.N · A2.1 Title]` (drawings first, ~40k characters), with the
  instruction to quote the sheet and point out what disagrees, and to send
  Coy to the sheet under the section. What is drawn but not written isn't
  in it, and he says so.
- **Call notes → Dusty's quote (Coy, 2026-10-03: "make notes on a live
  call so that Dusty can finish putting the quote together from aerial
  imagery afterwards").** **Call notes** on the person page and the customer
  page opens a pad (`call-notes-pad.tsx`, drawn at the page's top level) that
  saves as Coy types: the first words make a `contact_notes` row
  (`POST /api/people/[id]/notes`), each pause updates it
  (`PATCH /api/notes/[id]`), and a copy stays on the device until the server
  has it. **Quo sends a call only after hang-up** (no ringing event reaches
  us — measured 2026-10-03), so the note waits on the person; once the call
  arrives it is attached to the call it was written during —
  `callForNote()` (`src/lib/call-notes.ts`, tested): the call's own window
  from Quo's `call.completed` (`createdAt` → `completedAt`, raw log §2a),
  from 2 minutes before it rang to 15 minutes after hang-up, a note during a
  call beating the tail of the one before. A note in no window stays a plain
  note; a call with no window takes none. Attaching is done by the reads that
  show notes (the pad, the person and customer timelines, Dusty's job
  context — `settleCallNotes()`, session client, notes from the last three
  days with no call yet, no migration). The pad then says "On the 3:54 PM
  call" with **Draft the quote**, also on every call card:
  `POST /api/messages/[id]/quote` finds the call's job, else the caller's or
  their customer's newest open job — or, on Coy's yes, starts one — opens
  it, and puts the question in Dusty's box for Coy to send. Dusty's job
  context carries **Calls about this job** (the newest four with summary,
  next steps and Coy's notes on each; transcripts of the newest two, marked
  machine-heard, Coy's notes winning) beside the Plan Room's measured
  aerial, and he answers with a budget plan (§3) Coy applies. A live
  call shows on its thread since 2026-10-06 (Incoming alerts, below).
- **Texted photos onto the job (Coy, 2026-10-03: "she texted pictures. I
  would like to be able to pull those pictures into the plan room so that I
  can compare them to the aerial … into the budget and plan room and
  proposal").** A texted photo stays in `message_media` (the text is never
  changed); **+ Job** copies it into the job's files —
  `/api/jobs/[id]/texted-photos` (GET the photos texted by the job's person
  and their customer's other people, each with `file_id` once on the job;

  category photo, **In plan room**, Dusty reads it), named "Texted by

  A photo not yet stored is first copied in from Quo (`captureMedia()`,
  `src/lib/message-media-server.ts`, shared with the thread's media route).
  As a job file it can be set to an area (shows under that section's
  Photos) and picked as the proposal's cover. **Where:** the job page's

  (the section PATCH now keeps 8,000 characters of it, 2,000 of the Notes). A final freezes it with its

Now the button reads ✓ Used and a line under it says "✓ Saved as …'s scope of work" (on a phone "Close the chat to see it"), or, when the answer held no bullets (or terms) to use, says so in red; a page's `apply` returns false for that (`DustyUseTarget`, `dusty-live.ts`).
